---
name: merged-skills-registry
description: Use when checking whether upstream third-party skills have new content to fold into local custom skills, when adding a new merge entry to the registry, or when running an audit of folded skills. The registry lists every third-party skill whose content has been operationally merged into a self-authored vault skill, with the upstream URL and the local target. Audits diff upstream against local and surface non-trivial deltas for triage. Trigger phrases include "check upstream for skill updates", "is the X skill still up to date", "audit merged skills", "time to refresh upstream sources", "fold new upstream content into Y".
metadata:
  version: 1.81.0
---

# Merged Skills Registry

> **Skill marker**: When applying this skill, begin your reply with `[skill: merged-skills-registry]` on its own line so the transcript shows the skill fired. If multiple skills fire on the same reply, emit each marker on its own line at the top: transparency over neatness.

## Overview

A single source of truth for third-party skills whose content has been operationally folded into a local custom skill in this vault.

The vault has three integration patterns for upstream skills:

1. **Adopt as-is.** A faithful, verbatim copy of an upstream skill. Normally added via `add-skill.sh` (git sparse-checkout) and refreshed by `update-skill.sh`; sources that are not a sparse-checkout target (for example a skill file shipped inside a package repo) are refreshed by `check-pattern1.sh`. Tracked in the "Pattern 1: adopt-as-is register" section below plus the machine-readable `pattern1-skills.tsv`, not in the main merge table.
2. **Customise from upstream at adoption.** Fetch upstream, edit, write a local body. `.sources/<name>` reads `local CLAUDE.md`. Registered here.
3. **Fold into an existing local skill.** Upstream content merged into a local target's body. The third-party folder may be deleted entirely; the upstream URL lives only here.

The main merge table below is for patterns 2 and 3. Pattern 1 has its own register and version-drift tooling (see "Pattern 1: adopt-as-is register").

**Self-authored MCP-wrapper skills are out of scope for this registry.** The vault carries three self-authored wrappers over wired MCPs (`asana-workflows`, `calendar-workflows`, `gmail-workflows`) and may add more as MCPs come online (e.g. a future `drive-workflows` once Box / OneDrive / SharePoint are wired). These have no upstream third-party skill to track; they are first-party MCP discipline, not folds. They live alongside the folded entries below but do not need registry rows.

**Core principle:** when upstream evolves, you should be able to ask "is anything new I should know?" and get a structured answer per merged skill.

Upstream's successor file adds a "name the break" falsifiability gate (name the production change that would make the test fail before writing the body), the change-detector rule (a test only intentional decisions can fail fires on redesign and sleeps through bugs), behaviour-not-text (asserting a script or config CONTAINS a line proves only that the source is the source; run the artefact and assert outputs, which matters here because this vault is itself a corpus of agent-instructing documents), the mutation check, your-code-not-the-framework, make-doubles-specific, and seven new warning signs. Landed: Iron Laws 4 and 5; the new design-time section (name-the-break, no change detectors, behaviour-not-text, your-code-not-the-framework, gate function); a "Completeness is not specificity" subsection under Anti-Pattern 4 (per-branch doubles, assert arguments / counts / ordering); a "The mutation check" section; 5 new Quick Reference rows; and Red Flags split into mock-side and design-side. Vault-original framing at three points: behaviour-not-text is called out as mattering more here than in a typical codebase because the vault IS a corpus of agent-instructing documents, which is exactly where a grep-the-text test looks reasonable and verifies nothing; the mutation check is tied back to change detectors (a change detector survives every mutation on the list, so the check doubles as an after-the-fact detector for it); and the Bottom Line widened from mocks-only to both principles. testing-anti-patterns 1.0.1 to 1.1.0. |

The gap this closed: local's per-task workflow said only "if issues: implementer fixes, re-review", with no cap, no resume-vs-fresh rule and no escalation, so a task the implementer could not see its way out of would spin indefinitely with nothing in the process saying stop. Landed as a new "The fix loop is bounded" subsection placed after "Two-stage review, in order" (the loop is what follows a failed review, so it belongs there, and the placement keeps the locked two-stage ordering intact rather than replacing it): five-round cap; rounds 1-3 resume the ORIGINAL implementer, rounds 4-5 a fresh implementer one model tier up with the "a prior implementer attempted this N times" framing; the scoped re-review (ADDRESSED / NOT ADDRESSED per finding with file:line, "attempted" is not addressed, new breakage inside the fix diff ONLY, out-of-scope observations to the ledger so they cannot extend the loop); the two pre-loop exits (minors never enter; a plan-conflicting finding is the user's decision); controller-never-fixes-findings-itself; the breaker as a 3-row adjudication table with adjudicate-only-at-the-cap and no-silent-discard; plus the final-review shape (ONE fix wave, exactly one scoped re-review, no second wave, residuals to Layer 4). Ledger section gained the per-fix-round line, framed on WHY it matters here: the round counter that bounds the loop is exactly the state that does not survive compaction. Red Flags gained a 7-row fix-loop rationalisation table. VAULT ADAPTATION: upstream's resume step is harness-dependent and it hedges with a "if your harness cannot message a live subagent" fallback; this harness CAN, via SendMessage to the agent id from the original Agent call, so the fold names that mechanism directly and adds the requirement to record the agent id at dispatch time, keeping the fallback only as the id-is-gone case. | `incident-response-network` | `https://github.com/vahagn-madatyan/netsec-skills-suite` | `skills/incident-response-network` | Customised at adoption | Stage 4 network-forensics specialist for security incidents. Six-step procedure (evidence preservation, initial triage, lateral movement detection, containment verification, timeline reconstruction, post-incident documentation). Two threshold tables (Evidence Priority Classification with 7 evidence types ranked by volatility; Containment Verification Criteria with ACL counter / null route / VLAN isolation / flow-record checks). One decision tree (Evidence Collection Priority with active-vs-post-incident routing and time-window-based ARP / MAC / flow / syslog branches). Full four-way Cisco vendor-tag breakout applied to forensics commands: `[IOS]` / `[IOS-XE]` / `[NX-OS]` / `[IOS-XR]` plus `[JunOS]` / `[EOS]`. The four Cisco platforms diverge meaningfully and the upstream's flat `[Cisco]` tag is replaced at vault: classic IOS lacks `monitor capture` (use legacy `debug ip packet detail acl <name>` or `ip traffic-export`); NX-OS uses `show ip arp` (not bare `show arp`), `show ip access-lists` (not bare `show access-lists`), `ethanalyzer local interface <intf>` (not `monitor capture`), and saves tech-support to `bootflash:` (not `flash:`); IOS-XR drops `ip` from many shows (`show arp`, `show route`, `show access-lists`, `show logging`), uses `show l2vpn forwarding bridge-domain mac-address` for L2VPN CAM cases (pure L3 routers have no CAM table), and EPC has platform-dependent support (ASR9k yes, CRS-1 limited, NCS5500 yes); tech-support on IOS-XR bundles into `harddisk:tech-<hostname>-<date>.tgz` compressed archive. Both upstream references folded into body in full (methodology not lookup-table; per C-3 plan principle 2): `cli-reference.md` (149 lines, 8 command categories) folded across Step 1 (terminal session logging into preamble; packet capture into 1b; flow verification into 1d; ARP / MAC / routing into 1a + 1c; tech-support into 1e), Prerequisites (syslog and SNMP verification), Step 4 (ACL hit counts); `forensics-workflow.md` (213 lines, volatility ordering + artifact taxonomy + chain-of-custody + timeline methodology) folded into a new "Evidence handling" section between Prerequisites and Procedure (volatility table plus chain-of-custody record template plus per-platform SHA-256 hash commands) plus Step 5 (5-substep timeline reconstruction methodology: anchor events, timestamp normalisation, correlation by shared attributes, gap identification, chronological assembly with phase annotation). Em dashes removed; British / Pacific English. Vault sections added: skill marker (per locked wording), Initial Assessment with 3 numbered design-context blocks (blast radius and incident scope, evidence-preservation discipline, containment authority and read-only stance), Do NOT use this skill for (carves out: general incident-response process management belongs in `incident-response-lifecycle` to be adopted in PR4 or `oncall-runbooks`; endpoint / malware / DFIR out of network scope; containment execution belongs in change-management not this read-only skill; routine network troubleshooting belongs in protocol-depth or `multi-vendor-network-ops`; general log search belongs in `siem-log-analysis` / `network-log-analysis` / `graylog-log-investigation`), Prerequisites delta (folded cli-reference content; per-vendor flow-export verification commands; per-vendor NTP-verification commands; terminal session logging cheatsheet), Common failure modes (renamed from upstream Troubleshooting; extended with two new vendor-specific failure modes: "NX-OS uses `ip arp` not `arp`" with the syntax correction, "IOS-XR EPC unavailable on legacy platforms" with span / mirror fallback guidance), Cross-references, Red flags (10-item catalogue), Bottom line. Vault-specific red flags catalogue: paste auth key / session token / credential in evidence file shared outside IR team, packet capture without packet-count or duration limit (CPU saturation; especially NX-OS `ethanalyzer` on supervisor), declare containment effective on zero ACL counter delta, treat ARP-aged-out as "no host present" (4-hour ARP / 5-minute CAM aging), skip SHA-256 hash on collected evidence file (breaks chain of custody), log into device using shared admin account during forensics (attribution loss), execute containment from this read-only skill (containment requires change ticket + peer review + blast-radius assessment), run `show tech-support` on device under live attack without rate-limiting (10-100 MB output plus CPU-intensive; can tip device into unresponsiveness), trust collector timestamps when device NTP is broken (events timestamped at receive time not actual time), conflate this skill with `incident-response-lifecycle` (this is technical evidence layer; lifecycle is NIST 800-61 process layer; complementary not interchangeable). Cross-references to `incident-response-lifecycle` (NIST 800-61 process wrapper; to be adopted in PR4 C-3d), `oncall-runbooks` (devops-flavour sibling), `siem-log-analysis` (SIEM-equipped log evidence retrieval; landed in PR1), `network-log-analysis` (no-SIEM raw-syslog companion; landed in PR2), `acl-rule-analysis` (containment ACL design and rule-ordering), `cisco-firewall-audit` / `palo-alto-firewall-audit` / `fortigate-firewall-audit` / `checkpoint-firewall-audit` (firewall-side containment), IGP-trio plus `bgp-analysis` (routing-state forensics), `multi-vendor-network-ops` (9-element response contract), `systematic-debugging` (wider debugging loop), `secrets-hygiene` (credential redaction in evidence files), `utc-timestamps` (Step 5 timeline normalisation), `cite-sources` (external threat-intel citations), `completion-gate` Layer 3 (post-containment verification), `humanise-comms` (mixed-audience reports). License preserved (Apache-2.0). |
| `incident-response-lifecycle` | `https://github.com/vahagn-madatyan/netsec-skills-suite` | `skills/incident-response-lifecycle` | Customised at adoption | Stage 4 NIST 800-61 incident-response process-management specialist. Vendor-agnostic; no platform tags. Six-step procedure (detection and classification, triage and escalation, investigation coordination, communication management, resolution and recovery, post-incident review). Three threshold tables (Severity Classification Matrix P1-P4 with user-impact / service-impact / data-risk / response-SLA columns; Escalation and Role Matrix with IC / Tech Lead / Comms Lead / Scribe assignment per severity; Enhanced Monitoring Duration with monitoring period / alert threshold reduction / re-escalation trigger per severity). Two decision trees (Incident Severity Assignment with service-availability and data-exposure branches; Escalation Decision with role-assignment and vendor-engagement routing). Four-role model: Incident Commander (owns end-to-end, makes escalation decisions, single point of authority), Technical Lead (coordinates diagnostics, synthesises findings from parallel investigation tracks), Communications Lead (drafts stakeholder notifications, manages status page, shields IC and Tech Lead from comms overhead), Scribe (maintains real-time timeline, records bridge call decisions; the source of truth for post-mortem timeline reconstruction). Selective `references/` keep per C-3 plan principle 2 (lookup-table content): `communication-templates.md` (222 lines, audience-specific notification templates for executive / technical / customer / regulatory plus status update cadence templates plus bridge call meeting templates plus closure notification template plus post-mortem invitation template) kept at `incident-response-lifecycle/references/communication-templates.md`; `rca-framework.md` (252 lines, 5-whys facilitation guide with worked examples plus fishbone diagram template plus contributing-factor categorisation process/people/technology plus four-disposition action-item model fix/mitigate/accept/transfer plus post-mortem document structure) kept at `incident-response-lifecycle/references/rca-framework.md`. Both references received em-dash purge (24 each pre-purge to 0) and US-to-UK spelling cleanup (behavior to behaviour, analyze to analyse, organize to organise, prioritize to prioritise, recognize to recognise, minimize to minimise, normalize to normalise, summarize to summarise, finalize to finalise, characterize to characterise, emphasize to emphasise, specialize to specialise, utilize to utilise, realize to realise, center to centre, defense to defence, offense to offence, organization to organisation, authorize to authorise, color to colour, plus categorize / categorization to categorise / categorisation) applied at adoption. Em dashes in body removed; British / Pacific English. Vault sections added: skill marker (per locked wording), Initial Assessment with 3 numbered design-context blocks (severity-criteria policy alignment, contact-directory readiness, communication channels inventory), Do NOT use this skill for (carves out: network-level evidence collection belongs in `incident-response-network`; generic devops on-call discipline belongs in `oncall-runbooks`; log evidence retrieval belongs in `siem-log-analysis` / `network-log-analysis` / `graylog-log-investigation`; routine change management belongs in change-management process directly; endpoint / application IR belongs in domain-specific process), Prerequisites delta (incident management authority, contact directory, communication channels, incident tracking system, defined severity criteria), Common failure modes (renamed from upstream Troubleshooting; extended with "IC and Technical Lead combined at P1" failure mode), Cross-references, Red flags (10-item catalogue), Bottom line. Vault-specific red flags catalogue: assign IC and Tech Lead to same person at P1 (single-person bottleneck), skip the Scribe role at P1 (timeline gaps from memory reconstruction), 5-whys ending at a person rather than a system condition (blameless-culture iron rule), post-mortem held more than 7 days after resolution (memory decay), action items without owners and due dates, send executive update with raw technical detail, send customer-facing comms with internal hypothesis language, declare resolved before enhanced monitoring period elapses, conflate this skill with `incident-response-network` (process vs technical evidence; complementary not interchangeable), send regulatory notification draft for review by the IC who is also in the bridge call (overloads IC; separate review path via legal / compliance / executive sign-off). Cross-references to `incident-response-network` (network-forensics technical evidence arm; Step 3 explicitly hands evidence collection to it), `oncall-runbooks` (devops-flavour sibling), `siem-log-analysis` (SIEM-equipped log evidence retrieval; Step 3), `network-log-analysis` (no-SIEM raw-syslog alternative; Step 3), `humanise-comms` (Step 4 stakeholder communications tone), `utc-timestamps` (Step 6 timeline-of-events and MTTD/MTTI/MTTR metrics), `systematic-debugging` (Step 3 hypothesis tracking discipline), `completion-gate` Layer 3 (Step 5 recovery validation), `secrets-hygiene` (regulatory comms must not leak credentials), `cite-sources` (post-incident report external indicator citations), `plan-time-tooling` (engineering:incident-response mandatory trigger), `multi-vendor-network-ops` (9-element response contract on production-impacting actions during recovery). License preserved (Apache-2.0). |

Six-step procedure (log collection assessment, syslog pattern recognition, event correlation, anomaly detection, timeline reconstruction, report). Two decision trees (Investigation Entry Point with trigger-type routing, Anomaly Classification). Full four-way Cisco vendor-tag breakout applied to syslog message formats: `[IOS]` / `[IOS-XE]` / `[NX-OS]` / `[IOS-XR]` plus `[JunOS]` / `[EOS]`. The four Cisco platforms diverge meaningfully: classic IOS is largely identical to IOS-XE for common mnemonics but lacks newer facilities (IOSXE_INFRA-*, APP_HOSTING-*); NX-OS uses `%module-severity-MNEMONIC` style with module names like ETHPORT / ETH_PORT_CHANNEL / VPC / FEX rather than IOS-XE facility names like LINK / LINEPROTO; IOS-XR prefixes every message with `RP/<rack>/<slot>/CPU<n>:` plus process name and uses different facility prefixes (`PKT_INFRA-LINK-3-UPDOWN`, `ROUTING-BGP-5-ADJCHANGE`, `ISIS-6-ADJCHG`) so regex against IOS-XR logs needs prefix-awareness or the message body will not match. `cli-reference.md` (267 lines, rsyslog / syslog-ng configuration, grep / awk / sed one-liners, log-parsing pipelines) folded across Prerequisites, Step 1 preamble (collector configuration cheatsheet for both rsyslog and syslog-ng), Step 3 (correlation helpers with epoch-conversion awk pattern), Step 5 (timestamp-normalisation pattern). `syslog-patterns.md` kept at `network-log-analysis/references/syslog-patterns.md` (lookup-table-shaped content per selective-keep principle from C-3 plan); extended with four new vendor sections (classic Cisco IOS, Cisco NX-OS, Cisco IOS-XR each with their own facility code tables and critical-mnemonic catalogues; classic IOS section notes the operational identity with IOS-XE plus the facility absences); all four event-pattern tables (Interface, Authentication, Configuration Change, Routing Adjacency) expanded from upstream's 3-vendor columns to 6-vendor columns matching the four-way breakout; new IS-IS adjacency row added (covers SP-core deployments); new EIGRP row added with N/A notation for non-Cisco vendors and IOS-XR (EIGRP not implemented on XR); new "NX-OS and IOS-XR specific events" supplementary table covering vPC peer-keepalive failure, FEX offline, process-restart facility differences. Em dashes removed; British / Pacific English. Vault sections added: skill marker (per locked wording), Initial Assessment with 3 numbered design-context blocks (collector topology, log rotation cadence + retention, NTP synchronisation baseline), Do NOT use this skill for (carves out SIEM-equipped investigation, general-purpose Graylog, endpoint / application / cloud-audit-trail logs, collector-configuration-from-scratch, real-time alerting, packet-level forensics), Prerequisites delta (folded cli-reference collector-configuration content; per-vendor NTP-verification commands; multi-vendor authentication-failure clustering one-liners), Common failure modes (renamed from upstream Troubleshooting; extended with two new vendor-specific failure modes: "IOS-XR prefix breaks regex" with sed-strip-then-grep pattern, "NX-OS module facility differs from IOS-XE" with module-name-based pattern guidance), Cross-references, Red flags (10-item catalogue), Bottom line. Vault-specific red flags catalogue: grep on rotated log without `.gz` tail check, awk on multi-line stack trace without record-separator change, sort timeline before timestamp normalisation to UTC, trust device-local log buffer for events older than ring, `tail -f` on NFS-mounted log file, regex against IOS-XR log without `RP/0/RP0/CPU0:` prefix awareness, treat NX-OS facility code as identical to IOS-XE (`LINK-3-UPDOWN` does not exist on NX-OS; equivalent is `%ETHPORT-5-IF_DOWN_LINK_FAILURE`), paste full syslog message bearing session token into shared report, build "real-time" alert from polling grep loop (use SIEM or zabbix-templates-and-triage), recommend containment from this skill (investigation skill not response skill; hand off to incident-response-network and change-management skills). Public-domain content used: SQLite official docs (PRAGMA reference, WAL mode, STRICT tables semantics, type affinity rules, ALTER TABLE limitations, the 12-step table-recreation pattern, online backup API, VACUUM INTO, RETURNING). Sections cover iron rules (NEVER SQLite over NFS / SMB / network FS, four base pragmas WAL + foreign_keys + busy_timeout + synchronous=NORMAL on every connection, one writer at a time, INTEGER PRIMARY KEY full-word for rowid alias, money in INTEGER cents not REAL, backup via VACUUM INTO not cp); type affinity table per use-case (Integer PK / Boolean / Timestamps as ISO 8601 UTC TEXT / UUID / Money / JSON with json_valid CHECK); connection pragmas (four-line baseline plus optional cache_size / temp_store / mmap_size); concurrency model (one writer + many readers in WAL; serialised write connection + read pool for multi-threaded apps; SQLITE_BUSY remediation with long-running-read-transaction root cause); schema discipline (FK-on per connection, CHECK constraints worked example, STRICT tables 3.37+ with allowed-types list, WITHOUT ROWID selective use with do-NOT-with-INTEGER-PK rule, generated columns 3.31+ VIRTUAL vs STORED with index implications); indexes (FK index always, composite ordering equality-then-range, partial index for status='pending' patterns, EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN as the gate); transactions (BEGIN IMMEDIATE for writes to avoid lock-upgrade SQLITE_BUSY, BEGIN DEFERRED for reads, SAVEPOINT for nested rollback, RETURNING 3.35+); migrations and the ALTER TABLE limitation (table of supported vs unsupported operations with version cutoffs, the 12-step table-recreation pattern, application migration tooling discipline with PRAGMA locking_mode = EXCLUSIVE + real-schema-read-not-SELECT-1 + flock cooperation); backup discipline (VACUUM INTO default, online backup API alternative, cp --reflink=always after wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE) for migration backups, NEVER plain-copy an open database); VACUUM and maintenance (auto_vacuum=INCREMENTAL must be set before any tables are created, PRAGMA optimize on connect or close, full VACUUM is an outage on large databases); 14-row anti-patterns table including the SQLite-over-NFS rule and the SELECT-1-does-not-lock gotcha; 13-item new-schema checklist; edge-SQLite ecosystem brief (Cloudflare D1 / Litestream / rqlite / libSQL / Turso) with caveats; verification checklist mapped onto `completion-gate` Layer 3; cross-refs to `mysql-best-practices`, `mariadb-deltas`, `postgres-best-practices`, `forward-compatible-schemas` (ALTER TABLE limitations make additive-only discipline MORE important not less), `secrets-hygiene` (SQLite has no privilege model; access control is filesystem permissions; DB file owner and group matter), `plan-time-tooling`, `systematic-debugging` (SQLITE_BUSY four-phase loop), `oncall-runbooks` (database-is-locked runbook), `bash-defensive` (backup automation), `utc-timestamps` (strftime '%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ' is the SQLite-standard ISO 8601 UTC format), `linux-host-ops` (filesystem permissions, backups, log rotation, disk-space monitoring); 13-item red-flags list including SQLite-over-NFS, REAL-for-money, cp-on-live-db, INT-PRIMARY-KEY-not-INTEGER, BEGIN-instead-of-BEGIN-IMMEDIATE, auto_vacuum-set-too-late, SELECT-1-as-lock-noop, no-backup-automation, edge-SQLite-assumed-vanilla. License: Apache-2.0 (vault default for self-authored). |

Six rules applied to both branches: throwaway-from-day-one with clear naming and co-location, one-command-to-run, no-persistence-by-default, skip-the-polish, surface-the-state, delete-or-absorb-when-done. Capture-the-answer discipline (commit message, ADR, issue, NOTES.md) tied to downstream skills. Adopted as-is plus voice cleanup pattern: em dashes removed (replaced with semicolons or parentheses); upstream UI-branch hard-coupling to web-stack ("URL search param and a floating bottom bar") softened to project-appropriate variant toggle (URL parameter, env var, CLI flag, route segment, framework-idiomatic mechanism); upstream branch routing via separate `LOGIC.md` / `UI.md` files inlined as Logic Branch and UI Branch sections (vault avoids broken-link references to separate files); upstream `pnpm <name> / python <path> / bun <path>` task-runner examples extended with `cargo run --example` and `make prototype` for cross-stack coverage; upstream description expanded with vault trigger-phrase pattern; added Initial Assessment section with CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md preamble; added Cross-references section pointing to `systematic-debugging` (prototype as Phase 1 feedback loop), `improve-codebase-architecture`, `to-prd`, `to-issues`, `greedy-with-constraints`, `tdd`. License: MIT (upstream root; preserved on adoption). **REFRESH RESOLVED + DIVERGENCE LOCKED 2026-08-16 (operator decision, monthly audit): the logic-branch artifact stays the terminal-app shape; upstream's 2026-08-05 shareable-HTML-demo shape (6bcbcb0) is DECLINED and locked, do not re-derive. Separately, upstream's rule-6 change from "delete or absorb" to "capture the prototype as a primary source" WAS adopted this pass (commit the throwaway to a branch off main, leave a pointer, record the verdict), aligning with the estate's capture-durably discipline; prototype 1.0.0 to 1.1.0.** | 2026-05-16 |

## Pattern 1: adopt-as-is register

Adopt-as-is skills are verbatim copies of an upstream skill with no local fold. Because the local copy IS the upstream, their update question is not "has the upstream prose drifted from our fold" (the question the main merge table and the audit cron answer) but simply "has upstream shipped a newer release; if so, re-pull verbatim". That is a version check, not a content diff, so it has its own small toolchain rather than riding the prose-drift audit.

**Tracking.** Human-readable provenance lives in `.sources/<name>` (as for every skill). Machine-readable state lives in `pattern1-skills.tsv` at the vault root, consumed by `check-pattern1.sh`, `list-skills.sh`, and `update-skill.sh`. Columns: `name`, `check_type` (`pypi` or `git-dir`), `upstream_id`, `version_file` (the file whose contents are the pinned version; the single source of truth for the pin), `repo`, `repo_path`. Append columns at the end; never reorder or rename.

**Tooling.**

- `./check-pattern1.sh` reports version drift for every registered pattern-1 skill (read-only; safe for cron). Exit non-zero if any skill trails upstream or cannot be resolved.
- `./check-pattern1.sh --refresh <name>` re-pulls one skill from upstream and updates its pin (user-confirmed; not run automatically).
- `list-skills.sh` tags pattern-1 skills with `[pattern-1: pinned <version>]`.
- `update-skill.sh` redirects `pypi` pattern-1 skills to `check-pattern1.sh --refresh` rather than attempting a sparse-checkout that would fail.

**Cadence.** The monthly cron (`vault-audit-monthly`) runs `check-pattern1.sh` and surfaces any drift for user-confirmed refresh. Refresh is never automatic.

**Register.**

| Skill | check_type | Upstream | Pinned | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|

_No pattern-1 (adopt-as-is) skills are currently registered._ The `graphify` skill (upstream `safishamsi/graphify`, package `graphifyy`) was removed wholesale on 2026-07-28 (user decision: drop rather than maintain the version pin; it was the only pattern-1 skill and had drifted to 0.9.17). The tooling above (`check-pattern1.sh`, `pattern1-skills.tsv`) stays in place for any future adopt-as-is skill.

## How to add a new merge entry

When you fold a third-party skill into a local one (or customise one at adoption):

1. Add a row to the table above with the local skill name, upstream URL, upstream path, pattern, and a one-line note.
2. Decide whether to keep the third-party `SKILL.md` body in the vault:
   - **Delete the folder** when the local target supersedes operationally and you never want the third-party version to fire alongside.
   - **Keep the folder** when the third-party skill is still genuinely useful standalone (e.g. it is broader than your local customisation).
3. Decide whether to keep the `.sources/<third-party-name>` line:
   - **Keep** if you want `update-skill.sh` to be able to pull future upstream changes for diffing.
   - **Delete** if the folder was deleted and you do not need scriptable refresh; the registry holds the upstream URL.
4. Commit the registry edit alongside the other changes.

### Skill marker convention (every authored or adopted skill)

Every SKILL.md in this vault carries a one-line directive instructing the agent to begin any reply driven by that skill with `[skill: <name>]` on its own line. This gives transcript-level visibility into which installed skill is pulling weight on a given reply.

Placement: immediately after the H1 and any intro paragraphs, before the first H2 section. Wording (locked):

```markdown
> **Skill marker**: When applying this skill, begin your reply with `[skill: <name>]` on its own line so the transcript shows the skill fired. If multiple skills fire on the same reply, emit each marker on its own line at the top: transparency over neatness.
```

Where `<name>` is the literal directory name (which equals the frontmatter `name:` value). Applies to every skill in the vault including always-on (`humanise-comms`, `cite-sources`, `utc-timestamps`, `secrets-hygiene`) and meta-skills (`author-skill`, `merged-skills-registry`, `plan-time-tooling`, etc.); the user chose full transparency at the Chunk 4 decision point.

When folding a new third-party skill or authoring a new local one, include this directive at the canonical placement before opening the PR. The `_docs/skill-review-queue.md` snapshot for Chunk 4 (PR #71) records the rollout across all 78 vault skills.

## Audit procedure

When you (or the user) say "audit merged skills" or any equivalent trigger phrase:

1. For each row in the registry, fetch the current upstream `SKILL.md`. Use `WebFetch` against the source repo's raw URL (substitute the path), or `update-skill.sh` if the third-party folder still exists in the vault.
2. Diff the fetched upstream against the local target's body.
3. Categorise each delta:
   - **Operational guidance the user has not seen** (new rule, new pattern, new red flag).
   - **Example refresh only** (no new rule, just better worded examples).
   - **Conflicts with the user's customisation** (upstream changed; local diverges deliberately).
4. Surface the operational and conflict items via `AskUserQuestion`. Each delta gets one question with options: incorporate / dismiss / defer.
5. Skip the example-refresh items unless the user asks.
6. Write the user's decisions back into the relevant local SKILL.md files (small edits) or the registry (when the decision is "diverge deliberately, note the rationale").

## Cadence

- **On demand:** any of the trigger phrases. Overrides the schedule.
- **Monthly, and it is the only scheduled cadence (per upstream):** each upstream's next-audit date = (last fold date OR last audit date) + 30 days. Audit fires when the date is in the past. EVERY registered upstream sits here; there is no faster tier.
- **Weekly tier: RETIRED 2026-08-16.** See the retirement note in the next subsection. `vault-audit-weekly` and its cron are gone; do not re-add a faster tier without re-reading that note first.
- **Before adopting a NEW upstream as Pattern 2 or 3:** check the registry first to see if a related skill is already folded; the new upstream might fold into the same local target.
- **Pattern-1 version drift (monthly):** the monthly cron also runs `check-pattern1.sh`, reporting any adopt-as-is skill whose pinned version trails upstream. Read-only in the cron; refresh stays user-confirmed via `check-pattern1.sh --refresh <name>`. See "Pattern 1: adopt-as-is register".

The previous "weekly idle pass" rule (replaced 2026-05-19 in vault hygiene Chunk 6 B-2.5) over-fired: B-1 surfaced zero deltas for tikoci, B-2 surfaced 10 deltas across 4 upstreams (8 from netclaw, 2 from chrishuffman, 0 from vahagn / olandodeflexy). Most upstreams are operationally static week-over-week; monthly default plus a weekly exception list matches the observed signal without burning sub-agent budget on no-delta runs.

### Weekly cadence: RETIRED 2026-08-16 (operator decision)

**There is one scheduled cadence now, the monthly one, and it covers every registered upstream.** The
frequent-use table that used to sit here named four upstreams (`obra/superpowers`, `getsentry/skills`,
`anthropics/claude-code`, `mattpocock/skills`) for a 7-day window. It is gone, along with the
`vault-audit-weekly` scheduled task and its cron.

**The evidence, because a future pass will be tempted to re-add a faster tier.** The weekly cron never landed
a single content fold unattended across its whole recorded history. The fires on 2026-07-30, 2026-08-06 and
2026-08-14 each landed zero. That is structural rather than unlucky: Step 3 of the audit protocol requires a
per-delta `AskUserQuestion` confirmation before any fold, and a cron run has nobody to ask, so the expensive
half of a weekly pass (clone, sub-agent delta analysis, triage) can only ever queue defers for a user-present
pass to action later. The 34 folds recorded on 2026-07-22 came from a pass with the user present, not from
the cron acting alone. A faster cadence on work that cannot land is a faster queue, not faster progress.

**What the weekly could act on alone is preserved, not lost.** The one output a cron can land without
confirmation is bookkeeping correctness: a dead or moved fold source, repointed. That is the existence probe,
it is cheap (one HTTP status call per folded path, no clones, no sub-agents), and it now runs as **Step 2a of
the monthly task**, which had never carried it. Note the largest upstream move ever caught here, the
`chrishuffman5/domain-expert` 67-file restructure, was found by the MONTHLY fire on 2026-08-09, on a fire
where the cadence had deferred every single upstream.

**The accepted cost, stated so nobody rediscovers it as a surprise.** Detection latency on a moved fold path
rises to at most 30 days. Judged acceptable because a 404 is a state and not an event: it does not decay, and
nothing reads the stale pointer in the interim.

**Precedent.** `xixu-me/skills` was demoted from this same table on 2026-07-22 for near-identical reasoning,
that the weekly slot was spending effort on an upstream which structurally could not yield deltas. This
decision generalises that finding from one upstream to the tier itself.

**Before re-promoting anything to a faster tier, the bar is evidence that the tier can ACT**, not evidence
that an upstream moves often. An upstream shipping weekly releases is not an argument for a weekly audit
while folds still require confirmation. The argument would change if the protocol ever allowed a class of
fold to land unattended, or if a user-present pass ran on a reliable weekly rhythm.

**Historical note on the demoted `xixu-me/skills` row.** Its 2026-07-22 demotion rationale is preserved
because it is a good worked example of the "upstream structurally cannot yield deltas" test: its
`github-actions-docs` is a documentation ROUTER whose references file is a bare list of `docs.github.com`
URLs, so a repo-wide grep for `job_workflow_ref` returned zero hits and two consecutive weekly passes
produced nothing. Link rot is not something a fold audit can absorb.


### Audit-pass marker convention (so a run can detect its own gaps)

Every audit run appends a summary to a representative row per upstream, opening with a fixed, greppable phrase:

```
Weekly audit pass YYYY-MM-DD (vault-audit-weekly):
Monthly audit pass YYYY-MM-DD (vault-audit-monthly):
```

Grep the registry for `audit pass 20` to list every recorded pass in date order. A run should do this at Step 1 and compare the newest marker against its own cadence: a marker more than about 30 days old means one or more fires produced no bookkeeping, and the run should say so in its closure rather than silently starting a fresh window. **`Weekly audit pass` markers are HISTORICAL** (2026-07-22 through 2026-08-14) and no new ones will be written, since that cadence retired on 2026-08-16; they stay in the rows as the audit trail and should be read as evidence of what was checked when, never as a live cadence. Only `Monthly audit pass YYYY-MM-DD (vault-audit-monthly):` is written from now on.

**Probe a folded path for EXISTENCE as well as for commits (added 2026-08-06).** The per-folded-path probe
`gh api repos/<r>/commits?path=<p>&since=<last marker>` returns an empty list for a path that no longer
exists, which is byte-identical to the answer it gives for a genuinely quiet path. So a DELETED or RENAMED
fold source reads as NO_DRIFT forever, and the staler it gets the quieter it looks. Pair every `since` probe
with an existence check and treat a non-200 as a finding:

```
curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' -H "Authorization: token $GH_TOKEN" \
  "https://api.github.com/repos/<r>/contents/<p>"
```

Check the status code, not a `jq` field: `gh api ... --jq '.name'` fails on a DIRECTORY path (the response is
an array), so a jq-based check reports every folded directory as missing and buries the real 404s in false
positives. The 2026-08-06 pass found seven mattpocock paths at 404, two of them dead since 2026-07-02, which
the 2026-07-30 pass had recorded as "zero commits on all 10 folded paths".

**A moved fold source can move the WHOLE upstream, not one path (added 2026-08-09).** `chrishuffman5/domain-expert` restructured its entire tree from `skills/<domain>/<...>/<leaf>` into a plugin marketplace layout, `plugins/<domain>/skills/<leaf>`, flattening every intermediate directory. That is 73 registry path tokens across 36 rows, 160 `.sources/` tokens across 58 files, and 8 skill-body references, all dead at once, and none of it shows up as prose drift. The repoint landed on 2026-08-09. Three rules worth keeping for the next one:

1. **Resolve against the upstream tree, not by hand.** One `git/trees/<sha>?recursive=1` call returns the whole tree; map each old path by taking its deepest segment that exists as a real skill directory and re-hanging the remainder off it. Assert every rewritten target is present in that tree before writing, so a wrong pointer cannot land.
2. **Version-pinned subskills became reference files.** `<leaf>/<version>/SKILL.md` is now `<leaf>/references/versions/<version>.md`, so those 17 paths fail a naive flattening and would have been written as dead links. Zero version-pinned directories remain anywhere under `plugins/`.
3. **A multi-upstream row needs per-upstream path attribution.** Rows naming chrishuffman5 alongside netclaw or vahagn-madatyan carry all three upstreams' paths in one cell, and only the chrishuffman5-shaped ones move. Matching on `skills/<known-domain>/` separated them cleanly; matching on the bare `skills/` prefix would have corrupted 103 tokens belonging to other upstreams.

This convention was added on 2026-07-22 after exactly that failure. The cron had been enabled and firing since 2026-05-20, yet the registry held no greppable marker and the private-vault ledger no closure entry for any pass between 2026-05-20 and 2026-07-22, so the gap was invisible to every run in between. Marker text is the detection surface; do not drop it, even on an audit-pass-no-deltas run.

### How to add, defer or drop an upstream

There is one scheduled task and it derives its scope from this file at fire time:

- `vault-audit-monthly` (cron `0 13 9 * *`; 9th of every month 13:00 SGT) walks the main "Merge entries"
  table, takes every distinct registered upstream, and audits those whose next-audit date is in the past.

It carries no copy of the list, so maintenance is single-touch:

1. **Add an upstream (it becomes auditable):** register it in the main "Merge entries" table with its
   upstream URL, the folded path, and the local target. The next monthly fire picks it up automatically.
   Add its `.sources/<skill>` pointer in the same change, since the probe derives folded paths from there.
2. **Defer an upstream (audit it less often):** there is no faster or slower tier to move it to. Push its
   recorded next-audit date out in its row, and the cadence rule does the rest. Say why in the row's Notes
   cell, so a later pass can tell a deliberate deferral from a stalled one.
3. **Drop an upstream entirely (no more audits at all):** remove its rows from the main "Merge entries"
   table AND any references in the local skills' `.sources/` pointers (delete the pointer file if the
   upstream was its only source). The next monthly fire skips it because it is no longer registered. Verify
   with `mcp__scheduled-tasks__list_scheduled_tasks` that no manual task references the dropped upstream.

**Do not re-add a weekly tier without reading the retirement note above.** The bar is evidence that a faster
tier can ACT on what it finds, not that an upstream changes often.

The scheduled task fires only while Claude Code is open; if closed at fire time, it fires on next launch.
Manage it via the Scheduled section in the sidebar or `mcp__scheduled-tasks__list_scheduled_tasks` (use
`update_scheduled_task` to edit the prompt or the cron expression; do NOT edit the prompt to re-add a
hard-coded upstream list, which would reintroduce the duplication this section closes).

### Future: signal-based cadence (deferred)

Time-based cadence above is the immediate rule. The right long-term input is per-session skill-load telemetry: high-load skills audited more often, low-load skills moved past monthly to quarterly. Note the 2026-08-16 retirement finding constrains this too: a faster tier is only worth building if it can ACT on what it finds, so telemetry would need to arrive alongside a class of fold that lands without per-delta confirmation, not on its own. Requires: per-session skill-load logging, aggregation pipeline, automated registry-feedback loop. None of that infrastructure exists today. Tracked as a follow-up in the vault-hygiene roadmap; revisit when a wider telemetry chunk lands.

## When NOT to use this skill

- For Pattern-1 skills (clean clones), use `update-skill.sh` directly.
- For self-authored skills with no upstream (`.sources/<name>` reads `local CLAUDE.md` and there is no upstream URL anywhere), there is nothing to audit.

## Red Flags

- A folded skill with no registry row.
- A registry row with no upstream URL.
- Audit pass run silently (master incorporates upstream changes without surfacing the diff).
- A local skill that has obviously diverged from upstream but no row recording the divergence rationale.

## Bottom Line

Folded upstreams need bookkeeping or you lose the ability to refresh them. The registry is that bookkeeping. Audits are structured, not silent.
